## 1. Overview: Two Replication Systems

Unreal Engine 5 contains two different network replication systems:

- **Actor Replication (Classic / Legacy)** — the original system, built around
  `UNetDriver` + `UActorChannel` + `FObjectReplicator`. Mature, default, supported
  everywhere. Scales poorly with many connections because state diffing is per
  connection.
- **Iris** — a newer, data-oriented system (experimental in UE5.1, maturing since).
  Centralized `UReplicationSystem`, push-based dirtiness, cache-friendly data layout.
  Designed to scale to large player counts / many replicated objects.

**API compatibility:** Iris was designed to be largely drop-in compatible with
existing `UPROPERTY(Replicated)` declarations. `GetLifetimeReplicatedProps`,
`DOREPLIFETIME`, and `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY` work in both systems.

### Quick comparison

| Aspect             | Actor Replication (Classic)        | Iris                              |
|--------------------|------------------------------------|-----------------------------------|
| Architecture       | Per-actor channels (`UActorChannel`) | Centralized `UReplicationSystem`  |
| Dirty detection    | Comparison / polling (per connection) | Push-based (mark dirty once)     |
| Data layout        | Object-oriented, scattered         | Data-oriented, cache-friendly     |
| CPU scaling        | Scales poorly with many connections | Scales much better               |
| Maturity           | Fully mature, default              | Newer (was experimental, maturing)|
| API to declare     | `GetLifetimeReplicatedProps` / `DOREPLIFETIME` | Same macros + NetSerializers |

---

## 2. Classic Actor Replication — Core Model

Built around: **`UNetDriver` + `UActorChannel` + `FObjectReplicator`**

### Per-tick flow

```text
UNetDriver::TickFlush()
  └─ ServerReplicateActors()                 // WHAT to send, to WHOM
       └─ for each UNetConnection
            └─ for each relevant actor
                 └─ UActorChannel::ReplicateActor()
                      └─ FObjectReplicator::ReplicateProperties()  // diff + serialize
                           └─ writes changed properties into an FOutBunch
```

Then bunches get packed into packets and sent over the wire.

### Key actors

- **`UNetDriver`** — the orchestrator. One per world (the "GameNetDriver").
  - Owns the list of `UNetConnection` objects (one per connected client).
  - Drives the per-tick replication loop via `TickFlush()` → `ServerReplicateActors()`.
  - Maintains network relevancy + prioritization (`NetPriority`) and bandwidth budgeting.
  - Owns the optional `UReplicationGraph` (relevancy/prioritization) and the
    `FNetGUIDCache` (object ↔ NetGUID mapping so both ends agree on object identity).
  - `ServerReplicateActors()` decides what to replicate this frame: dormancy checks,
    relevancy checks, prioritization, and per-frame actor budget.

- **`UNetConnection`** — one client's link. One per connected player on the server.
  - Owns the set of open channels (multiplexed over the single connection).
  - Manages the `FOutBunch`/`FInBunch` flow: packs bunches into UDP packets, handles
    ordering, reliability, ACKs, resends.
  - Holds per-connection state (the `ActorChannels` map: Actor → its `UActorChannel`).
  - **Why classic scales poorly:** state diffing happens *per connection*. If 100
    players see the same actor, the "what changed" work is largely repeated 100 times.

- **`UActorChannel`** — the actor's pipe on a connection. One per (Actor, Connection).
  - If Actor A is relevant to 3 connections, there are 3 `UActorChannel`s for it.
  - Owns the `FObjectReplicator` for the actor *and* one per replicated subobject/component.
  - `ReplicateActor()` is the heart: asks each `FObjectReplicator` to serialize changed
    properties, writes spawn info on first replication, bundles into an `FOutBunch`.
  - Handles actor lifecycle over the network: opening the channel = "spawn on client",
    closing = "destroy / make irrelevant". Routes incoming RPCs and property updates.
  - Other channel types: `UControlChannel` (channel 0, handshake/login), `UVoiceChannel`.

- **`FObjectReplicator`** — the property diffing engine for a single `UObject`.
  - Holds a per-connection shadow/"retirement" buffer (last-sent values).
  - `ReplicateProperties()` walks the object's replicated properties (driven by the
    `FRepLayout`), compares current values to the shadow buffer, and serializes changed
    properties (value + property handle) into the bunch.
  - Handles RepNotify (`OnRep_` functions) and conditional replication (`COND_*`).

### Supporting cast

- **`FRepLayout`** — a cached, flattened description of a class's replicated properties
  (offsets, types, array handling, conditions). Built once per class and shared. Makes
  the property walk fast.
- **`FRepState` / `FRepChangelistState`** — per-object/per-connection change tracking
  (shadow data, history of changelists for reliability).
- **`FRepChangedPropertyTracker`** — tracks active/inactive property conditions and (for
  push model) dirty flags.

### How a property flows through

```cpp
// In the header
UPROPERTY(ReplicatedUsing = OnRep_Health)
float Health;

// In the .cpp
void AMyActor::GetLifetimeReplicatedProps(TArray<FLifetimeProperty>& OutLifetimeProps) const
{
    Super::GetLifetimeReplicatedProps(OutLifetimeProps);
    DOREPLIFETIME(AMyActor, Health);
}
```

1. `GetLifetimeReplicatedProps` is read once per class to build the `FRepLayout` and the
   list of `FLifetimeProperty` (the `COND_*` condition + push setting per property).
2. Each tick, for each connection where the actor is relevant,
   `FObjectReplicator::ReplicateProperties()` diffs `Health` against that connection's
   shadow copy.
3. If it changed → serialize the new value + handle into the `FOutBunch`.
4. On the client, the value is applied and `OnRep_Health` is fired.

### Cost characteristic

Default = **poll/compare**: every replication pass re-reads *every* replicated property
and *compares* it to the shadow buffer to discover changes. This is
**O(connections × replicated_properties)** of comparison work.

### Structural summary

```text
UNetDriver  (1 per world)
 ├─ FNetGUIDCache              (object ↔ NetGUID mapping)
 ├─ UReplicationGraph?         (relevancy/prioritization, optional)
 └─ UNetConnection  (1 per client)
     ├─ FOutBunch/FInBunch packing, ACKs, reliability
     └─ UActorChannel  (1 per Actor on this connection)
         ├─ FObjectReplicator  (for the Actor)
         │    ├─ FRepLayout         (shared per class: flattened prop list)
         │    ├─ Shadow buffer      (last-sent values)
         │    └─ ReplicateProperties() → diff → FOutBunch
         └─ FObjectReplicator  (for each replicated component/subobject)
```

---

## 3. Push Model (Classic Optimization & Bridge to Iris)

Push Model optimizes the *comparison* part of classic replication. Two parts must
**both** be in place: build/config, and code (declare + mark dirty).

### Step 1 — Build / config flags

**Build flag (compile-time):**
```csharp
// In YourGame.Target.cs / YourGameEditor.Target.cs
bWithPushModel = true;
```
Enabled by default for editor and development builds; typically compiled out of shipping
unless you opt in.

**CVar (runtime):**
```ini
; DefaultEngine.ini
[SystemSettings]
net.IsPushModelEnabled=1
```
Or at runtime in the console:
```text
net.IsPushModelEnabled 1
```
This is the master switch. With it `0`, even push-marked properties fall back to comparison.

### Step 2 — Declare properties as push-based

Canonical example pattern (mirrors `APlayerState`):

```cpp
void APlayerState::GetLifetimeReplicatedProps(TArray<FLifetimeProperty>& OutLifetimeProps) const
{
    Super::GetLifetimeReplicatedProps(OutLifetimeProps);

    FDoRepLifetimeParams SharedParams;
    SharedParams.bIsPushBased = true;                    // opt this property into push model

    DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST(APlayerState, Score,             SharedParams);
    DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST(APlayerState, bIsSpectator,      SharedParams);
    DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST(APlayerState, PlayerNamePrivate, SharedParams);

    // Reuse the params struct and tweak conditions per-property:
    SharedParams.Condition = COND_SkipOwner;
    DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST(APlayerState, CompressedPing,    SharedParams);

    SharedParams.Condition = COND_InitialOnly;
    DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST(APlayerState, PlayerId,          SharedParams);
}
```

Differences from classic:
- `FDoRepLifetimeParams` with `bIsPushBased = true`.
- `DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST` (the `_FAST` variant) instead of plain `DOREPLIFETIME`.

The header declaration is unchanged:
```cpp
UPROPERTY(ReplicatedUsing = OnRep_Score)
float Score;
```

### Step 3 — Mark the property dirty whenever you change it (CRUCIAL)

With push model, the engine **no longer compares** the value to a shadow copy. It assumes
unchanged *unless you tell it otherwise*. Forget to mark dirty → it will not replicate.

```cpp
void APlayerState::SetScore(float NewScore)
{
    if (Score != NewScore)
    {
        Score = NewScore;
        MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_FROM_NAME(APlayerState, Score, this);
    }
}
```

### Available macros

| Macro | When to use |
|---|---|
| `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_FROM_NAME(ClassName, PropertyName, Object)` | Most common; class + property by name. |
| `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY(Object, Property)` | When you already have the `FProperty*`. |
| `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_UNSAFE(Object, RepIndex)` | Low-level; supply the rep index directly. |
| `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_FROM_NAME_STATIC_ARRAY_INDEX(...)` | Mark a specific index of a static array property. |

### Minimal full example

```cpp
// MyActor.h
UCLASS()
class AMyActor : public AActor
{
    GENERATED_BODY()
public:
    AMyActor() { bReplicates = true; }

    void SetHealth(float NewHealth);

    UPROPERTY(ReplicatedUsing = OnRep_Health)
    float Health = 100.f;

    UFUNCTION()
    void OnRep_Health();

    virtual void GetLifetimeReplicatedProps(TArray<FLifetimeProperty>&) const override;
};
```

```cpp
// MyActor.cpp
#include "Net/UnrealNetwork.h"
#include "Net/Core/PushModel/PushModel.h"   // for MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY

void AMyActor::GetLifetimeReplicatedProps(TArray<FLifetimeProperty>& OutLifetimeProps) const
{
    Super::GetLifetimeReplicatedProps(OutLifetimeProps);

    FDoRepLifetimeParams Params;
    Params.bIsPushBased = true;
    DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST(AMyActor, Health, Params);
}

void AMyActor::SetHealth(float NewHealth)
{
    if (HasAuthority() && Health != NewHealth)   // only authority changes replicated state
    {
        Health = NewHealth;
        MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_FROM_NAME(AMyActor, Health, this);
    }
}
```

### Under the hood

- The comparison is **skipped**; a dirty bitfield (`FRepChangedPropertyTracker` / net push
  dirty-state) is set by `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY`.
- `ReplicateProperties()` only serializes properties whose dirty bit is set; the relevant
  state is cleared after sending.
- Win: the expensive per-property comparison is replaced by O(1) "is this bit set?" checks.
  Matters most for objects with many properties and/or many connections.

### Common pitfalls

1. **Forgetting `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY`** → the property silently never replicates (#1 bug).
2. **Mixing** — must mark dirty in *every* place the value is mutated (incl. array adds).
3. **Mutating from non-authority** — only the authority's marks matter.
4. **Build vs. CVar mismatch** — if off, marks are no-ops and it falls back to comparison
   (so it still works, which can mask missing marks during testing!).

### Verify it's on

- Console: `net.IsPushModelEnabled` (prints current value).
- Net profiling/validation CVars can compare push results against the comparison path to
  catch missing dirty marks.

---

## 4. Iris Replication — Core Model

Where classic is **per-(actor, connection)** and **object-oriented**, Iris is
**centralized** and **data-oriented**. The mental model shifts from
*"each actor's channel diffs itself per connection"* to
*"one system holds all replicated state as data and computes what to send to whom in
batched passes."*

### The big structural inversion

Classic:
```text
UNetDriver → UNetConnection → UActorChannel(per actor) → FObjectReplicator(per object) → diff
```

Iris:
```text
UReplicationSystem (1, central)
   ├─ NetRefHandleManager         (all replicated objects as data / "NetObjects")
   ├─ ReplicationStateDescriptors (per-class flattened state layout)
   ├─ ReplicationStateStorage     (the actual quantized state buffers)
   ├─ Filtering / Prioritization / Conditionals (batched bitset passes)
   └─ ReplicationWriter/Reader    (per connection — pack the already-computed deltas)
```

The actor channel is **gone** as the unit of work. There's no per-actor
`FObjectReplicator` doing per-connection comparisons.

### Per-frame pipeline

1. **Poll/copy** dirty state from game objects into Iris state buffers.
2. **Quantize** the state (convert to the network representation) — once per object,
   *not* per connection.
3. **Filtering** — which objects are relevant to which connections (bitsets).
4. **Prioritization** — order objects by importance under the bandwidth budget.
5. **Serialization** — write the deltas per connection via the `ReplicationWriter`.

Steps 1–2 happen **once per object**, not once per (object × connection). This is the key win.

### Key concepts

- **`UReplicationSystem`** — the central brain. Owns the subsystems and drives the
  per-frame pipeline. (There can be more than one conceptually, but it's *the* owner of
  all replication.)

- **`FNetRefHandle` / `FNetRefHandleManager`** — objects as data.
  - Every replicated object gets a lightweight `FNetRefHandle` (an index, not a pointer).
  - The manager keeps a dense, contiguous array of "NetObjects" → cache-coherent,
    parallel-friendly iteration. "All replicated objects" is a flat table, not a graph of
    channels. This is what makes filtering/prioritization fast (bitset math over arrays).

- **`FReplicationStateDescriptor`** — the Iris analog of `FRepLayout`. Built per class/struct
  by the `ReplicationStateDescriptorBuilder`. Describes each replicated member + its
  NetSerializer, change-mask bit positions (dirty bits), and conditionals. Richer than
  `FRepLayout` because it also drives quantization. Generated once per type and shared.

- **`FNetSerializer`** — the per-type serialization unit. A declarative, reusable object
  that knows how to **Quantize / Dequantize / Serialize / Deserialize / IsEqual** one type
  (e.g. `float`, `FVector`, `FRotator`, enums, structs). `UPROPERTY(Replicated)` members
  match to NetSerializers when the descriptor is built; custom types declare one via the
  Net serialization macros. The **Quantize** step converts state to its compact network
  form **once**, stored, then the same quantized bytes are written to every connection that
  needs it (vs. classic, where serialization happens per channel).

- **Change mask (push is mandatory)** — in classic, push model was opt-in; in Iris,
  dirty tracking is the **foundation**.
  - Each NetObject has a change mask (a bitset, one region per replicated member/fragment).
  - Game code marks state dirty (the same `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY` macros bridge into Iris) →
    bits flip. Poll copies only dirty members; serialization only writes set bits.
  - There is a polling/compat path, but the architecture is built around the change mask,
    not per-connection shadow-buffer comparison.

- **Filtering / Prioritization / Conditionals** — batched, centralized passes (replace
  per-connection relevancy logic from `ServerReplicateActors`).
  - **Filtering** (`NetObjectFilter` / `ReplicationFiltering`): operates over the NetObject
    array with bitsets per connection ("which of these N objects does connection C get?").
    Includes distance/grid filters, owner filters, group filters. Bitset math over arrays
    scales much better than per-actor `IsNetRelevantFor` virtual calls.
  - **Prioritization** (`NetObjectPrioritizer`): assigns priorities so the bandwidth budget
    is spent on the most important objects.
  - **Conditionals** (`ReplicationConditionals`): `COND_OwnerOnly`/`COND_SkipOwner`/etc.
    become condition bits evaluated centrally.

- **`FReplicationWriter` / `FReplicationReader`** — the only strongly per-connection piece.
  - The `FReplicationWriter` (one per connection) takes the filtered + prioritized list of
    dirty NetObjects and writes their quantized, change-masked state into the connection's
    data stream, respecting the bandwidth budget. Tracks per-connection ack/resend state.
  - The `FReplicationReader` applies incoming state into the client's NetObject state, then
    dequantizes into the actual `UObject` members and fires RepNotifies.
  - Contrast with classic: there the comparison itself was per connection (expensive). Here
    the comparison/quantization already happened once centrally; the per-connection step is
    "select bits and pack" — much cheaper.

- **`UObjectReplicationBridge`** — glues Actors/Components ↔ Iris.
  - Registers `AActor`/`UActorComponent` instances as NetObjects (assigning `FNetRefHandle`s).
  - Translates engine actor lifecycle (BeginPlay/Destroy, dormancy, subobjects) to Iris
    NetObject create/destroy.
  - Routes RPCs through Iris's reliable/unreliable channels while keeping the familiar
    `Server`/`Client`/`Multicast` UFUNCTION semantics. This is why gameplay code mostly
    doesn't change.

---

## 5. Side-by-Side Mapping

| Classic concept                          | Iris concept                              | Note |
|------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|------|
| `UNetDriver` (replication loop)          | `UReplicationSystem`                       | Centralized pipeline instead of per-conn loop |
| `FNetGUID` + identity                    | `FNetRefHandle` + `FNetRefHandleManager`   | Objects are array rows, not channels |
| `UActorChannel` (per actor/conn)         | *(gone)* — NetObject + per-conn writer     | No per-actor channel object |
| `FObjectReplicator` (per object/conn diff) | Change mask + `FReplicationStateDescriptor` | Diff is central + push-based |
| `FRepLayout`                             | `FReplicationStateDescriptor`              | Flattened, plus quantize info |
| ad-hoc property serialize                | `FNetSerializer` (Quantize/Serialize)      | Declarative per-type, quantize once |
| Push Model (opt-in)                      | Change mask (mandatory)                    | Same `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY` macros |
| relevancy / `NetPriority` / RepGraph     | `NetObjectFilter` / `NetObjectPrioritizer` | Batched bitset passes |
| `FOutBunch` per channel                  | `FReplicationWriter` per connection        | Packs pre-quantized state |
| —                                        | `UObjectReplicationBridge`                 | Glues actors ↔ Iris |

---

## 6. Key Takeaways

- **Classic** scales O(connections × properties) because the diff/serialize happens per
  (object × connection). Push Model optimizes the *comparison* but keeps the per-connection
  channel structure.
- **Iris** makes the work happen *per object once* (poll → quantize → mark dirty); only the
  cheap "pick bits + pack" step is per connection. That's the whole reason it scales to
  large player counts / many objects.
- **Compatibility:** `UPROPERTY(Replicated)`, `GetLifetimeReplicatedProps`, and
  `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY` work in *both* systems — the deliberate bridge. Everything learned
  about push model is Iris's normal operating mode.

---

## 7. Where to Read the Source (UE 5.8)

High-value files for studying Iris in the engine tree:

- `Engine/Source/Runtime/Net/Iris/Public/Iris/ReplicationSystem/ReplicationSystem.h`
  — the top-level brain.
- `Engine/Source/Runtime/Net/Iris/Public/Iris/ReplicationSystem/ReplicationSystemTypes.h`
  — handles/types.
- `Engine/Source/Runtime/Engine/Public/Net/Iris/ReplicationSystem/ReplicationSystemUtil.h`
  (and the `.cpp`) — the actor bridge layer.
- `ReplicationSystemTestPlugin` — readable isolated examples of registering objects,
  filtering, and the writer/reader (great for learning).

For classic replication + push model:
- `Net/Core/PushModel/PushModel.h` — the `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY` macros.
- `PlayerState.cpp` — canonical push-model `GetLifetimeReplicatedProps` example.
- `RepLayout.cpp`, `DataReplication.cpp` (`FObjectReplicator`), `NetConnection.cpp`,
  `ActorChannel.cpp`, `NetDriver.cpp`.
```

I've created the file at `Docs/Networking_Iris_vs_Actor.md` in your `AZ` project. It contains the complete discussion:

1. **Overview** of both systems with the quick-comparison table
2. **Classic Actor Replication** — the full `UNetDriver` + `UActorChannel` + `FObjectReplicator` model, per-tick flow, supporting types, and the structural diagram
3. **Push Model** — build flags, CVar, declaration pattern, `MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY` macros, full example, internals, and pitfalls
4. **Iris** — the structural inversion, per-frame pipeline, all key concepts (`UReplicationSystem`, `FNetRefHandle`, `FReplicationStateDescriptor`, `FNetSerializer`, change mask, filtering/prioritization, writer/reader, the bridge)
5. **Side-by-side mapping** cheat sheet
6. **Key takeaways**
7. **Where to read the source** in your UE 5.8 tree

```markdown
# UE5 Networking: Classic Actor Replication vs. Iris

A study summary comparing the two replication systems in Unreal Engine 5
(context: UE 5.8, built from source).

## 1. Overview
- Actor Replication (Classic): UNetDriver + UActorChannel + FObjectReplicator. Mature, default. Per-connection diffing → scales poorly with many connections.
- Iris: centralized UReplicationSystem, push-based, data-oriented. Scales to large player counts.
- API compatibility: UPROPERTY(Replicated), GetLifetimeReplicatedProps, DOREPLIFETIME, and MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY work in BOTH.

## 2. Classic Actor Replication — Core Model
Per-tick flow:
  UNetDriver::TickFlush() -> ServerReplicateActors()
    for each UNetConnection
      for each relevant actor
        UActorChannel::ReplicateActor()
          FObjectReplicator::ReplicateProperties()  // diff + serialize -> FOutBunch

Key actors:
- UNetDriver: one per world. Owns connections, drives per-tick loop, GuidCache (object<->NetGUID), optional ReplicationGraph.
- UNetConnection: one per client. Owns channels, packs bunches into packets, reliability/ordering/ACKs. Diffing is per connection.
- UActorChannel: one per (Actor, Connection). Owns FObjectReplicators, handles actor spawn/destroy over the wire.
- FObjectReplicator: property diff engine for one UObject. Per-connection shadow buffer of last-sent values; compares current vs. shadow.

Supporting types: FRepLayout (flattened per-class prop list), FRepState/FRepChangelistState, FRepChangedPropertyTracker.

Cost: default poll/compare = O(connections x properties).

## 3. Push Model (classic optimization, bridge to Iris)
Build/config:
  // *.Target.cs
  bWithPushModel = true;   // default on for editor/dev
  ; DefaultEngine.ini
  [SystemSettings]
  net.IsPushModelEnabled=1

Declare push-based:
  FDoRepLifetimeParams Params;
  Params.bIsPushBased = true;
  DOREPLIFETIME_WITH_PARAMS_FAST(AMyActor, Health, Params);

Mark dirty on EVERY change (crucial):
  Health = NewHealth;
  MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_FROM_NAME(AMyActor, Health, this);

Macros: MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_FROM_NAME, MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY, MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_UNSAFE,
MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY_FROM_NAME_STATIC_ARRAY_INDEX.

Under the hood: comparison skipped; dirty bitfield set by macro; only dirty props serialized.
Pitfalls: forgetting to mark dirty (silent no-replicate); mark in every mutation site; only authority's marks matter; build/CVar off falls back to comparison (masks missing marks).

## 4. Iris Replication — Core Model
Structural inversion:
  UReplicationSystem (1, central)
    NetRefHandleManager          (objects as data rows)
    ReplicationStateDescriptors  (per-class flattened layout)
    ReplicationStateStorage      (quantized state buffers)
    Filtering / Prioritization / Conditionals (batched bitset passes)
    ReplicationWriter/Reader     (per connection — pack pre-quantized state)

Per-frame pipeline: Poll/copy dirty -> Quantize (once per object) -> Filter -> Prioritize -> Serialize (per connection).
Steps 1-2 happen once per object, NOT per (object x connection) — the key win.

Key concepts:
- UReplicationSystem: central brain.
- FNetRefHandle / FNetRefHandleManager: objects as lightweight handles (indices) in dense arrays.
- FReplicationStateDescriptor: Iris analog of FRepLayout + quantize info + change-mask bits.
- FNetSerializer: declarative per-type unit (Quantize/Dequantize/Serialize/Deserialize/IsEqual). Quantize once, reuse bytes.
- Change mask: push dirtiness MANDATORY (foundation). Same MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY macros bridge in.
- Filtering/Prioritization/Conditionals: batched bitset passes.
- FReplicationWriter/Reader: only strongly per-connection piece; assembles packets from already-quantized state.
- UObjectReplicationBridge: glues Actors/Components <-> Iris NetObjects, handles lifecycle + RPCs.

## 5. Side-by-Side Mapping
| Classic | Iris |
| UNetDriver (loop) | UReplicationSystem |
| FNetGUID + identity | FNetRefHandle + FNetRefHandleManager |
| UActorChannel (per actor/conn) | (gone) NetObject + per-conn writer |
| FObjectReplicator (per object/conn) | Change mask + FReplicationStateDescriptor |
| FRepLayout | FReplicationStateDescriptor |
| ad-hoc property serialize | FNetSerializer (Quantize/Serialize) |
| Push Model (opt-in) | Change mask (mandatory) |
| relevancy / NetPriority / RepGraph | NetObjectFilter / NetObjectPrioritizer |
| FOutBunch per channel | FReplicationWriter per connection |
| - | UObjectReplicationBridge |

## 6. Key Takeaways
- Classic scales O(connections x properties); Push Model optimizes the comparison but keeps per-connection channels.
- Iris does poll -> quantize -> mark-dirty per object once; only "pick bits + pack" is per connection -> scales.
- Compatibility: replicated property macros + MARK_PROPERTY_DIRTY work in both systems.

## 7. Where to Read the Source (UE 5.8)
- Engine/Source/Runtime/Net/Iris/ ... ReplicationSystem.h, ReplicationSystemTypes.h
- Engine/Source/Runtime/Engine/Public/Net/Iris/ReplicationSystem/ReplicationSystemUtil.h
- ReplicationSystemTestPlugin (readable examples)
- Classic: Net/Core/PushModel/PushModel.h, PlayerState.cpp, RepLayout.cpp, DataReplication.cpp, NetConnection.cpp, ActorChannel.cpp, NetDriver.cpp
```

- **VS Code:** open the `.md`, install *Markdown PDF* extension, right-click → *Export (pdf)*.
- **Browser:** paste into a Markdown previewer → Print → Save as PDF.
